\Delta_{T}D^{h/q}

Table 1
:
Collinear quark semi-inclusive JFFs (left) and collinear FFs (right). The labels “U”, “L” and “T” in the header row represent unpolarized, longitudinally polarized and transversely polarized fragmenting quarks. And the labels in the header column represent the corresponding polarizations of the produced hadrons.

NLO calculation

Since the semi-inclusive JFFs
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}
describe the distribution of hadrons inside the jet, which contains hadronization/non-perturbative information, they are not perturbatively calculable.
In this respect, they are different from the purely perturbative semi-inclusive jet functions introduced in
[
KRV16b
]
.
However, we can still follow the standard perturbative QCD methodology and obtain the renormalization properties by evaluating the partonic jet fragmentation functions.
Hence, we will replace the hadron
h
by a parton
j
, and compute perturbatively compute
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}
as an expansion of the strong coupling constant
\alpha_{s}
.

Here we outline the calculation of the semi-inclusive JFFs for quark and gluon initiated jets
\mathcal{G}_{q,g}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}
, which have close relations to the conventional collinear FFs. For the unpolarized case, the collinear unpolarized JFFs
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}
is related to the collinear unpolarized FFs
D^{h/i}(z_{h},\mu)
as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}=\sum_{j}\int_{z_{h}}^{1}\frac{dz_{h}^{\prime}}{z_{h}^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}_{ij}(z,z_{h}^{\prime},\omega_{J},\mu)\,D^{h/j}\left(\frac{z_{h}}{z_{h}^{\prime}},\mu\right)\,,

(13)

where the coefficient functions
\mathcal{J}_{ij}
can be found in
[
KRV16a
]
. Note that we have selected
\omega_{J}=p_{JT}R
with the jet transverse momentum
p_{JT}
and the jet radius
R
, which are dependent on the jet kinematics. We have also included the renormalization scale
\mu
in the matching to collinear FFs. By studying the perturbative behavior of these JFFs, one can derive their renormalization group (RG) equations, which are the same as the usual time-like DGLAP evolution equations,

\displaystyle\mu\frac{d}{d\mu}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}=\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)}{\pi}\sum_{j}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{dz^{\prime}}{z^{\prime}}P_{ji}\left(\frac{z}{z^{\prime}},\mu\right)\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z', z_h, \omega_J, \mu}\,,

(14)

where
P_{ji}
are the splitting functions for unpolarized fragmentation functions
[
AP77
,
SV97
]
.

Next we turn to the polarized JFFs. The leading order polarized bare semi-inclusive JFF in the
\overline{\textrm{MS}}
scheme can be written as:

\Delta_{(T)}{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{j,(0)}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h}),

(15)

notice that
z
is equal to 1 because at LO the total energy of the initiating parton is transferred to the jet, and
z_{h}
is equal to one because the fragmenting parton inside the jet carries entire jet energy.

Figure 1
:

Feynman diagrams that contribute to the polarized semi-inclusive quark JFFs. As illustrated in (A), the quark initiating the jet has momentum
l=(l^{+},l^{-}=\omega,\bm{0}_{\perp})
with quark energy
\omega
as introduced in
eq.

5
. The black dashed lines are collinear quarks and red curly lines represent collinear gluons.

At the next-to-leading order (NLO) in SCET, the collinear processes will contribute, and the corresponding Feynman diagrams can be found in
[
KRV16b
,
KRV16a
]
. The semi-inclusive jet fragmentation functions (JFFs) are obtained by considering all possible final state cuts of the Feynman diagrams depicted in
fig.

1
. To carry out the calculations, we work in pure dimensional regularization with
d=4-2\epsilon
dimensions, focusing only on cuts through loops, where there are two final-state partons. The remaining cuts result in virtual contributions, leading to scaleless integrals that vanish in dimensional regularization. These virtual contributions primarily change infrared (IR) poles to ultraviolet (UV) poles, except for the IR poles that will eventually be matched onto the standard collinear fragmentation functions. Thus, in the end, we are left with UV poles only, which will be addressed through renormalization.

Considering the quark semi-inclusive JFF, we have two contributions to consider:
fig.

1
(A) and
fig.

1
(B)+(C), similar to the semi-inclusive jet function analyzed in a previous work
[
JPW11
,
CKR16
]
. Specifically, we focus on
\Delta_{(T)}\mathcal{G}_{q}^{q,(1)}
, where the incoming quark has momentum
l^{-}
, and the final-state quark has momentum
l^{-}-q^{-}
. Based on these momenta, we define the branching fraction
x=(l^{-}-q^{-})/l^{-}
. At this perturbative order, there are only two possibilities. First, if both the quark and the gluon are inside the jet, as illustrated in
fig.

2
(A), we have
z=1
and
z_{h}=x
. Second, if the gluon exits the jet, as shown in
fig.

2
(B), we have
z=x
and
z_{h}=1
. Extending the discussions from
[
KRV16a
]
, we consider both cases where both partons are in the jet and only one parton is in the jet:

Figure 2
:

Contributions required for studying semi-inclusive JFFs. In (A), one observes both the quark and the gluon inside the jet. In (B) and (C) only the quark or gluon is inside the jet, respectively.

1.

Both partons are inside the jet

The scenario depicted in
fig.

2
(A) corresponds to a quark-initiated jet. In this case, all the initial quark energy
\omega
is fully transferred to the jet, leading to
z=\omega_{J}/\omega=1
. However, the energy of the fragmenting parton, denoted by
\omega_{h}
, can be less than the jet energy, resulting in a general ratio
z_{h}=\omega_{h}/\omega_{J}<1
.

For the splitting process
i\rightarrow jk
, where
j
denotes the fragmenting parton, the one-loop bare semi-inclusive Jet Fragmentation Fragmentation Function (JFF) in the
\overline{MS}
scheme can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\Delta_{(T)}{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{jk,(1)}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{(e^{\gamma_{E}}\mu^{2})^{\epsilon}}{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)}\left(\delta(1-z)\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}(z_{h},\epsilon)\int\frac{dq_{\perp}^{2}}{(q_{\perp}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}}\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{\textrm{both}}\right)\,.

(16)

The superscript “
jk
” indicates that this is the contribution proportional to
\mathcal{\alpha_{s}}
where both partons
j
and
k
remain inside the jet. The term
\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}{\textrm{both}}
represents the constraints of the anti-
k_{T}
jet algorithm with both partons remaining inside the jet, and it is expressed using Heaviside functions:

\displaystyle\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{\textrm{both}}

\displaystyle=\theta\pqty{z_h (1 - z_h) \omega_J \tan{\frac{\mathcal{R}}{2}} - q_{\perp}}\,,\quad\mathcal{R}\equiv R/\cosh(\eta)\,.

(17)

2.

Only one parton is inside the jet

The scenario where one parton remains inside the jet while another parton exits the jet is depicted in
fig.

2
(B) and (C) for a quark-initiated jet. In this case, the final-state quark (or gluon) forms the jet with a jet energy
\omega_{J}=l^{-}-q^{-}=zl^{-}
. This means that only a fraction
z
of the incoming quark energy
\omega
is transferred to the jet energy. At this perturbative order, all the jet energy is fully transferred to the fragmenting parton inside the jet, leading to an overall delta function that enforces
z_{h}=\omega_{h}/\omega_{J}=1
.

It is crucial to distinguish this situation from earlier work
[
KRV16b
]
that considered the exclusive limit of the JFFs. In that case, an upper cut
\Lambda
was imposed on the total energy outside the measured jets to ensure an exclusive
n
-jet configuration. However, it was demonstrated explicitly in the context of angularities
[
EVW10
]
that for the exclusive case, this contribution is power suppressed as
\mathcal{O}(\Lambda/Q)
, where
Q
is the large scale of the process. In the present inclusive cross-section calculation, such constraints are not necessary, as we need to integrate over all momentum configurations similar to the case of fragmentation functions. Consequently, there are no power corrections of the form
\mathcal{O}(\Lambda/Q)
.

The constraints imposed by the jet algorithms require that one of the partons must be outside the jet, which can be formulated in the following manner for both cone and anti-
k_{T}
algorithms:

\displaystyle\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\theta\pqty{q_{\perp} - (1 - z) \omega_J \tan\frac{\mathcal{R}}{2}}\,,\quad\mathcal{R}\equiv R/\cosh(\eta)\,.

(18)

Note that this restriction is formulated in terms of the variable
z
, whereas the constraints in
eq.

17
are related to the variable
z_{h}
. Once again, we consider the splitting process
i\rightarrow jk
, where only parton
j
remains inside the jet and eventually fragments into the observed hadron.

This part of the bare semi-inclusive jet fragmentation functions can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\Delta_{(T)}{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{j(k),(1)}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{(e^{\gamma_{E}}\mu^{2})^{\epsilon}}{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)}\left(\delta(1-z_{h})\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}(z,\epsilon)\int\frac{dq_{\perp}^{2}}{(q_{\perp}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}}\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{j}\right)\,.

(19)

The superscript “
j(k)
” indicates that parton
k
exits the jet. The structure of this expression is similar to
eq.

16
, except for the presence of a different overall delta function and a distinct jet algorithm constraint.
\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{j}
.

Finally, by combining
eq.

16
and
eq.

19
, one obtains

\displaystyle\Delta_{(T)}{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{j,(1)}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{(e^{\gamma_{E}}\mu^{2})^{\epsilon}}{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)}\bigg(\delta(1-z)\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}(z_{h},\epsilon)\int\frac{dq_{\perp}^{2}}{(q_{\perp}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}}\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{\textrm{both}}

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z_{h})\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}(z,\epsilon)\int\frac{dq_{\perp}^{2}}{(q_{\perp}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}}\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{j}\bigg)\,,

(20)

where
\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{\textrm{both}}
and
\Theta^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}_{j}
are the anti-
k_{T}
jet algorithm constraints with both partons in jet and only one parton in jet as introduced in
eq.

17
and
eq.

18
.

The longitudinally polarized splitting functions
\Delta\hat{P}_{ji}(x,\epsilon)
in
eq.

20
are given in
[
Vog96
]
:

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{qq}(x,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=C_{F}\left[\frac{1+x^{2}}{1-x}-\epsilon(1-x)\right]\,,

(21)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{gq}(x,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=C_{F}\left[2-x+2\epsilon(1-x)\right]\,,

(22)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{qg}(x,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=T_{F}\left[2x-1-2\epsilon(1-x)\right]\,,

(23)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{gg}(x,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=2C_{A}\left[\frac{1}{1-x}-2x+1+2\epsilon(1-x)\right]\,,

(24)

and the transversely polarized splitting functions
\Delta_{T}\hat{P}_{ji}(x,\epsilon)
only exist for
\Delta_{T}\hat{P}_{qq}
and have been provided in
[
Vog98
]
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\hat{P}_{qq}(x,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=C_{F}\left(\frac{2x}{1-x}\right)\,.

(25)

After inserting the
\Theta
functions for anti-
k_{T}
algorithm and carrying out the integration in
eq.

20
, one obtains the bare results
for longitudinally polarized semi-inclusive JFFs
\Delta\mathcal{G}^{j}_{j,\text{bare}}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)
with
i,j\in\Bqty{q, g}
,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{G}^{q}_{q,\text{bare}}

\displaystyle(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=\,\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h})

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{qq}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{qq}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\right]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[2C_{F}(1+z_{h}^{2})\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z_h)}}{1-z_{h}}\right)_{+}+C_{F}(1-z_{h})+2\Delta P_{qq}(z_{h})\ln z_{h}\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[2C_{F}(1+z^{2})\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z)}}{1-z}\right)_{+}+C_{F}(1-z)\right]\,,

(26)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{G}^{g}_{q,\text{bare}}

\displaystyle(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{gq}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{gq}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\right]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[2\Delta P_{gq}(z_{h})\ln(z_h(1-z_h))-2C_{F}(1-z_{h})\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[2\Delta P_{gq}(z)\ln(1-z)-2C_{F}(1-z)\right]\,,

(27)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{G}^{q}_{g,\text{bare}}

\displaystyle(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{qg}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{qg}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\right]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[2\Delta P_{qg}(z_{h})\ln(z_h(1-z_h))+2T_{F}(1-z_{h})\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[2\Delta P_{qg}(z)\ln(1-z)+2T_{F}(1-z)\right]\,,

(28)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{G}^{g}_{g,\text{bare}}

\displaystyle(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=\,\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h})

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{gg}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta P_{gg}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\right]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg[4C_{A}\left(2(1-z_{h})^{2}+z_{h}\right)\left(\frac{\ln(1-z_h)}{1-z_{h}}\right)_{+}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg[2\Delta P_{gg}(z_{h})\ln z_{h}-4C_{A}(1-z_{h})\bigg]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[4C_{A}\left(2(1-z)^{2}+z\right)\left(\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}\right)_{+}-4C_{A}(1-z)\right]\,,

(29)

where
L\equiv\ln(\frac{\mu^2}{\omega_J^2 \tan[2](\mathcal{R}/2)})
.
The transversely polarized semi-inclusive JFFs are given by:

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\mathcal{G}^{q}_{q,\text{bare}}

\displaystyle(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)=\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h})

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+L\right)\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\right]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[4C_{F}z_{h}\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z_h)}}{1-z_{h}}\right)_{+}+2\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z_{h})\ln z_{h}\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\left[4C_{F}z\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z)}}{1-z}\right)_{+}\right]\,.

(30)

Here the functions
\Delta_{(T)}P_{ji}(z_{h})
are the longitudinally (transversely) polarized Altarelli-Parisi splitting kernels

\displaystyle\Delta{P}_{qq}(x)

\displaystyle=C_{F}\bqty{\frac{2}{(1-x)_+}-1-x+\frac{3}{2}\delta(1-x)},

(31)

\displaystyle\Delta{P}_{gq}(x)

\displaystyle=C_{F}\bqty{2-x},

(32)

\displaystyle\Delta{P}_{qg}(x)

\displaystyle=T_{F}\bqty{2x-1},

(33)

\displaystyle\Delta{P}_{gg}(x)

\displaystyle=2C_{A}\bqty{\frac{1}{(1-x)_+}-2x+1}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}\delta(1-x),

(34)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}{P}_{qq}(x)

\displaystyle=C_{F}\bqty{\frac{2x}{(1-x)_+} + \frac{3}{2} \delta(1-x)},

(35)

where
\beta_{0}\equiv\frac{11}{3}C_{A}-\frac{4}{3}T_{F}n_{f}
and
n_{f}
is number of flavors.
The “plus” distributions are defined as usual by:

\int_{0}^{1}\differential{z}f\pqty{z}\bqty{g \pqty{z}}_{+}=\int_{0}^{1}\differential{z}\pqty{f \pqty{z} - f \pqty{1}}g\pqty{z}.

(36)

Note that there is no gluon involved splitting for
\Delta_{T}
since there is no gluonic transversity distribution at leading twist.

It is important to point out that the
1/\epsilon
poles with a factor of
\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\pqty{z_h}\delta\pqty{1 - z}
in
eqs.

26
,
27
,
28
,
29
and
30
are IR poles that will be matched onto the standard longitudinally (transversely) polarized collinear FFs.
The poles with a factor of
\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\pqty{z}\delta\pqty{1 - z_h}
, on the other hand, are the UV poles which will be taken care of by renormalization.
Since the UV poles do not involve the variable
z_{h}
, we should expect that
z_{h}
is merely a parameter when doing the renormalization.
In matching onto the collinear FFs, however,
z_{h}
will become a relevant variable.
Both the renormalization and matching onto collinear FFs will be discussed in
section

1
.

Renormalization and matching onto collinear FFs

The subsequent action we will take involves the renormalization of the bare semi-inclusive JFFs obtained previously, followed by matching them onto the renormalized partonic fragmentation functions to address the IR poles.
The relationship between the bare and renormalized semi-inclusive JFFs is as follows:

\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i,\mathrm{bare}}^{j}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}=\sum_{k}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{\differential{z'}}{z^{\prime}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}Z_{ik}\pqty{\frac{z}{z'}, \mu}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j}\pqty{z', z_h, \omega_J, \mu},

(37)

where
\Delta_{\pqty{T}}Z_{ik}\pqty{\frac{z}{z'}, \mu}
is the renormalization matrix and
\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j}\pqty{z', z_h, \omega_J, \mu}
are the renormalized semi-inclusive JFFs.
As pointed out in
section

1
, the above convolution only involves the variable
z
.
The renormalized semi-inclusive JFFs satisfy the following RG evolution equations:

\mu\frac{\differential{}}{\differential{\mu}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}=\sum_{k}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{\differential{z'}}{z^{\prime}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\gamma_{ik}\pqty{\frac{z}{z'}, \mu}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j}\pqty{z', z_h, \omega_J, \mu},

(38)

where the anomalous dimension matrix is given by:

\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\gamma_{ik}=-\sum_{k}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{\differential{z'}}{z^{\prime}}\pqty{\Delta_{\pqty{T}} Z}_{ik}^{-1}\pqty{\frac{z}{z'}, \mu}\mu\frac{\differential{}}{\differential{\mu}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}Z_{kj}\pqty{z', \mu},

(39)

and
\pqty{\Delta_{\pqty{T}} Z}_{ik}^{-1}
is the inverse of the renormalization matrix that is defined such that it satisfies:

\sum_{k}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{\differential{z'}}{z^{\prime}}\pqty{\Delta_{\pqty{T}} Z}_{ik}^{-1}\pqty{\frac{z}{z'}, \mu}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}Z_{kj}\pqty{z', \mu}=\delta_{ij}\delta\pqty{1 - z}.

(40)

Up to
\order{\alpha_s}
, the renormalization matrix is

\Delta_{\pqty{T}}Z_{ij}\pqty{z, \mu}=\delta_{ij}\delta\pqty{1 - z}+\frac{\alpha_{s}\pqty{\mu}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}P_{ji}\pqty{z},

(41)

and therefore the anomalous dimension matrix is given by:

\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\gamma_{ij}\pqty{z, \mu}=\frac{\alpha_{s}\pqty{\mu}}{\pi}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}P_{ji}\pqty{z},

(42)

this suggests that the evolution of the renormalized polarized semi-inclusive JFFs conforms to the timelike DGLAP equation for collinear polarized FFs
[
AP77
]
:

\mu\frac{\differential{}}{\differential{\mu}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}=\frac{\alpha_{s}\pqty{\mu}}{\pi}\sum_{k}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{\differential{z'}}{z^{\prime}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}P_{ji}\pqty{\frac{z}{z'}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z', z_h, \omega_J, \mu}\,.

(43)

Notice that the hadronic JFFs have been reinstated, and the leading order splitting kernels are given in
eq.

31
-
35
.
An analogous finding was observed in the context of the semi-inclusive jet function in
[
KRV16b
]
.

Now that we have eliminated the UV poles from renormalization, we will still have to deal with the IR poles, which will be addressed by matching onto the collinear polarized FFs.
Such matching can be done at a scale
\mu\gg\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}}
as follows:

\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J, \mu}=\sum_{j}\int_{z_{h}}^{1}\frac{\differential{z_h'}}{z_{h}^{\prime}}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{J}_{ij}\pqty{z, z_h', \omega_J, \mu}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}D^{h/j}\pqty{\frac{z_h}{z_h'}, \mu},

(44)

where again, the hadronic semi-inclusive JFFs and collinear FFs are reinstated, and the relation is true up to a power correction of
\order{\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}}^2 / \pqty{\omega^2 \tan[2](\mathcal{R} / 2)}}

[
JPW11
,
CKR16
,
KRV16a
]
.
It should be noted that in this case, the variable being convolved is
z_{h}
, while
z
is merely a parameter.
This process is similar to the approach used for unpolarized JFFs described in
[
KRV16a
]
, except that instead of the unpolarized FFs, in this instance, collinear polarized FFs are applied

\Delta_{\pqty{T}}D^{j/i}\pqty{z_h, \mu}=\delta_{ij}\delta\pqty{1-z_h}+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\Delta_{\pqty{T}}P_{ji}\pqty{z_h}\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}.

(45)

Finally, the matching coefficients
\Delta_{\pqty{T}}\mathcal{J}_{ij}
for anti-
k_{T}
jet algorithm can be presented as follows

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{J}_{qq}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h})+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg\{L\bigg[\Delta P_{qq}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\Delta P_{qq}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\left[2C_{F}(1+z_{h}^{2})\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z_h)}}{1-z_{h}}\right)_{+}+C_{F}(1-z_{h})+\Delta\mathcal{I}_{qq}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}(z_{h})\right]

\displaystyle\left.-\delta(1-z_{h})\left[2C_{F}(1+z^{2})\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z)}}{1-z}\right)_{+}+C_{F}(1-z)\right]\right\}\,,

(46)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{J}_{qg}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg\{L\bigg[\Delta P_{gq}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\Delta P_{gq}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\left[2\Delta P_{gq}(z_{h})\ln(1-z_h)-2C_{F}(1-z_{h})+\Delta\mathcal{I}_{gq}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}(z_{h})\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\bigg[2\Delta P_{gq}(z)\ln(1-z)-2C_{F}(1-z)\bigg]\bigg\}

(47)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{J}_{gq}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg\{L\left[\Delta P_{qg}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\Delta P_{qg}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\right]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\left[2\Delta P_{qg}(z_{h})\ln(1-z_h)+2T_{F}(1-z_{h})+\Delta\mathcal{I}_{qg}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}(z_{h})\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\bigg[2\Delta P_{qg}(z)\ln(1-z)+2T_{F}(1-z)\bigg]\bigg\}\,,

(48)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{J}_{gg}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h})+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg\{L\bigg[\Delta P_{gg}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\Delta P_{gg}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\bigg[4C_{A}\left(2(1-z_{h})^{2}+z_{h}\right)\left(\frac{\ln(1-z_h)}{1-z_{h}}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-4C_{A}(1-z_{h})+\Delta\mathcal{I}_{gg}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}(z_{h})\bigg]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\left[4C_{A}\left(2(1-z)^{2}+z\right)\left(\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}\right)_{+}-4C_{A}(1-z)\right]\bigg\}\,,

(49)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\mathcal{J}_{qq}(z,z_{h},\omega_{J},\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z)\delta(1-z_{h})+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\bigg\{L\bigg[\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z)\delta(1-z_{h})-\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z_{h})\delta(1-z)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\delta(1-z)\left[4C_{F}z_{h}\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z_h)}}{1-z_{h}}\right)_{+}+\Delta_{T}\mathcal{I}_{qq}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}(z_{h})\right]

\displaystyle-\delta(1-z_{h})\left[4C_{F}z\left(\frac{\ln{(1-z)}}{1-z}\right)_{+}\right]\bigg\}\,,

(50)

where

\begin{split}\Delta\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{\mathrm{anti}-k_{T}}\pqty{z_h}&=2\Delta P_{ji}\pqty{z_h}\ln{z_h},\\
\Delta_{T}\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{\mathrm{anti}-k_{T}}\pqty{z_h}&=2\Delta_{T}P_{ji}\pqty{z_h}\ln{z_h}.\\
\end{split}

(51)

2
Collinear JFFs in exclusive jet productions

Exclusive jet production, such as proton-proton collisions leading to dijet events or electron-proton collisions producing electron-jet events, provides a valuable tool for understanding the fundamental dynamics of hadron structure and interactions. A key observable in these processes is the collinear jet fragmentation function, which describes the probability distribution for a parton in the jet to fragment into a particular hadron with a given momentum fraction.

The collinear jet fragmentation function plays a critical role in determining the properties of jets produced in these exclusive QCD processes, as it governs the hadronization of partons within the jet. This function is defined as the ratio of the differential cross-section for hadron-in-jet production to the differential cross-section for inclusive jet production. It depends on several variables, including the momentum fraction of the parton within the jet, the transverse momentum of the jet, and the factorization scale used to separate the hard and soft contributions to the jet production process.

Recent advances in perturbative QCD calculations and experimental measurements have led to a deeper understanding of the collinear jet fragmentation function and its role in exclusive QCD processes. In particular, studies have focused on the evolution of this function under renormalization group equations, which describe how it varies with changes in the factorization scale. Additionally, there has been increasing interest in the non-perturbative contributions to the collinear jet fragmentation function, which can be probed through experimental measurements of jet fragmentation functions.

In this section, we provide the derivation for the matching coefficients
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{J}_{ij}
of the exclusive jet fragmentation functions
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
with the collinear fragmentation function
\Delta_{(T)}D_{i}^{h}(z,\mu)
for anti-
{k}_{T}
jets.
These results were first written down in the appendix of
[
Waa12
]
, with which our results are consistent.
We start by specifying the phase space constraint from the jet algorithm, which was nicely outlined in
[
EVW10
]
.
Consider a parton splitting process,
i(\ell)\rightarrow j(q)+k(\ell-q)
, where an incoming parton
i
with momentum
\ell
splits into a parton
j
with momentum
q
and a parton
k
with momentum
\ell-q
.
The four-vector
\ell^{\mu}
can be decomposed in light-cone coordinates as
\ell^{\mu}=\pqty{\ell^+, \ell^- = \omega, 0_{\perp}}
where
\ell^{\pm}=\ell^{0}\mp\ell^{z}
.
The constraint for anti-
k_{T}
algorithm with radius
R
is given by:

\displaystyle\Theta_{\text{anti-}k_{T}}

\displaystyle=\theta\pqty{\tan[2](\frac{R}{2}) - \frac{q^+ \omega^2}{q^- \pqty{\omega- \ell^-}^2}}\,.

(52)

For jet fragmentation functions, the above constraint lead to constraint on the jet invariant mass
m_{J}^{2}=\omega\ell^{+}

[
PW12
]
, which is derived and listed as follows:

\displaystyle\delta_{\text{anti-}k_{T}}

\displaystyle=\theta\pqty{z(1-z) \omega^2 \tan[2](\frac{R}{2}) - m_J^2}\theta\pqty{m_J^2}\,,

(53)

where
z=q^{-}/\omega
.
The exclusive JFF
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
can be matched onto the fragmentation functions
\Delta_{(T)}D_{i}^{h}(z,\mu)
as:

\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)=\sum_{j}\int_{z}^{1}\frac{\differential{x}}{x}\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{J}_{ij}(\omega,R,x,\mu)\Delta_{(T)}D_{j}^{h}\pqty{\frac{z}{x}, \mu}+\order{\frac{\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}}^2}{\omega^2 \tan[2](R / 2)}}\,,

(54)

where
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{J}_{ij}
are the matching coefficients.
The exclusive JFFs
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{j}\pqty{m_J^2, z, \mu}
with
i,j\in\Bqty{q, g}
has been extensively studied in
[
JPW11
,
RW14
]
.
Using pure dimensional regularization with
4-2\epsilon
dimensions in the
\overline{\mathrm{MS}}
scheme, the bare results at
\order{\alpha_s}
can be written in the following compact form
[
GG92
,
RW14
,
CKR16
]
:

\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i,\text{bare}}^{j}\pqty{m_J^2, z}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{\pqty{e^{\gamma_E} \mu^2}^{\epsilon}}{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)}\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}(z,\epsilon)z^{-\epsilon}(1-z)^{-\epsilon}\pqty{m_J^2}^{-1-\epsilon}\,,

(55)

which is related to
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
in
eq.

54
by:

\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)=\int\differential{m_J^2}\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{m_J^2, z, \mu}\delta_{{\text{anti-}k_{T}}}\,,

(56)

notice that we reinstated hadronic JFFs.
The splitting functions
\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}(z,\epsilon)
are given in
eqs.

21
,
22
,
23
,
24
and
25
.
By inserting them into
eq.

54
and performing the integration over
m_{J}^{2}
with the constraints imposed by the jet algorithm
\delta_{\text{anti-}k_{T}}
, one obtains the bare exclusive JFFs
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i,\text{ bare }}^{j}(\omega,R,z)
.
We present the results for anti-
k_{T}
jets here, as their explicit expressions are not available in the literature:

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{q,\text{bare}}^{q}(\omega,R,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{\frac{1}{\epsilon^2} + \frac{3}{2 \epsilon} + \frac{L}{\epsilon}}\delta(1-z)-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bqty{\Delta P_{qq} (z) + \frac{3}{2} \delta(1-z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}+\Delta P_{qq}(z)(-L+2\ln{z})

\displaystyle\quad+(1-z)+2\pqty{1+z^2}\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\bigg\}\,,

(57)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{q,\text{bare}}^{g}(\omega,R,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Delta P_{gq}(z)+\Delta P_{gq}(z)\Big[-L+2\ln(z(1-z))\Big]-2(1-z)\bigg\}\,,

(58)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{g,\text{bare}}^{q}(\omega,R,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}T_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Delta P_{qg}(z)+\Delta P_{qg}(z)\Big[-L+2\ln(z(1-z))\Big]+2(1-z)\bigg\}\,,

(59)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{g,\text{bare}}^{g}(\omega,R,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{A}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{\frac{1}{\epsilon^2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon} \frac{\beta_0}{2 C_A}+\frac{L}{\epsilon}}\delta(1-z)+\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta P_{gg}(z)+\frac{\beta_0}{2 C_A} \delta(1-z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}+\Delta P_{gg}(z)(-L+2\ln{z})-4(1-z)

\displaystyle\quad+4\Big[2(1-z)^{2}+z\Big]\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\bigg\}\,,

(60)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\mathscr{G}_{q,\text{bare}}^{q}(\omega,R,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{\frac{1}{\epsilon^2}+\frac{3}{2 \epsilon}+\frac{L}{\epsilon}}\delta(1-z)+\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta_T P_{qq}(z)+\frac{3}{2} \delta(1-z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}+\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z)(-L+2\ln{z})+4z\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\bigg\}\,,

(61)

where, as given in the main text, one has
\beta_{0}
and
L
defined:

\beta_{0}\equiv\frac{11}{3}C_{A}-\frac{4}{3}T_{F}n_{f},\quad L=\ln(\frac{\mu^2}{\omega^2 \tan[2](R/2)})\,,

(62)

and
\Delta_{(T)}{P}_{ji}(z)
are given in
eq.

31
-
eq.

35
.
It is instructive to point out that the
\epsilon
poles in the first term of
eqs.

57
,
60
and
61
correspond to ultraviolet (UV) divergences, and they are related to the renormalization of the JFF
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i,\text{ bare}}^{j}(\omega,R,z)
.
All the remaining
\epsilon
poles in
eqs.

57
,
58
,
59
,
60
and
61
are infrared (IR) poles, and they match exactly with those in the fragmentation functions
\Delta_{(T)}D_{i}^{j}(z,\mu)
, which we will show below.

\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i,\text{bare}}^{h}(\omega,R,z)
is renormalized by:

\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i,\text{bare}}^{h}(\omega,R,z)=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)\,,

(63)

where
i
is not summed over in the above equation.
The corresponding renormalization group (RG) equation is given by:

\mu\frac{\differential{}}{\differential{\mu}}\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)=\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)\,,

(64)

where the anomalous dimension
\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)
is:

\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)=-\pqty{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathscr{G}}^i(\mu)}^{-1}\mu\frac{\differential{}}{\differential{\mu}}Z_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)\,.

(65)

The solution to
eq.

65
is then:

\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)=\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}\pqty{\omega, R, z, \mu_{\mathscr{G}}}\exp(\int_{\mu_{\mathscr{G}}}^{\mu} \frac{\dd{\mu'}}{\mu'} \gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^i \pqty{\mu'})\,,

(66)

where the scale
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}
should be the characteristic scale chosen such that large logarithms in the fixed-order calculation vanish.
The counter terms
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)
are given by

1
1

1

Note here the counter terms for polarized quark and gluon JFFs are the same as those of the unpolarized ones as shown in
[
CKR16
]
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathscr{G}}^{q}(\mu)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}C_{F}\bqty{\frac{1}{\epsilon^2}+\frac{3}{2 \epsilon}+\frac{L}{\epsilon}},

(67)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathscr{G}}^{g}(\mu)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}C_{A}\bqty{\frac{1}{\epsilon^2} + \frac{1}{\epsilon} \frac{\beta_0}{2 C_A} + \frac{L}{\epsilon}}\,.

(68)

From these results we obtain the anomalous dimension
\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)
with the following form:

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)=\Gamma_{\text{cusp}}^{i}\pqty{\alpha_s}\ln(\frac{\mu^2}{\omega^2 \tan[2](R/2)})+\gamma^{i}\pqty{\alpha_s}\,,

(69)

where
\Gamma_{\text{cusp}}^{i}=\sum_{n}\Gamma_{n-1}^{i}\pqty{\frac{\alpha_s}{4 \pi}}^{n}
and
\gamma^{i}=\sum_{n}\gamma_{n-1}^{i}\pqty{\frac{\alpha_s}{4 \pi}}^{n}
.
The lowest-order coefficients can be extracted from the above calculations:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{q}=4C_{F},

\displaystyle\quad\gamma_{0}^{q}=6C_{F},

(70)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{g}=4C_{A},

\displaystyle\quad\gamma_{0}^{g}=2\beta_{0}\,,

(71)

and higher-order results can be found in
[
JPW11
,
BNP07
,
BS10
,
EIS13a
,
MVV04
]
.
After the subtraction of the UV counter terms specified in
eqs.

67
and
68
, the renormalized JFF
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{j}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
are given by:

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{q}^{q}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta P_{qq}(z)+\frac{3}{2} \delta(1-z)}+\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta P_{qq}(z)(-L+2\ln{z})+(1-z)+2\pqty{1+z^2}\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\bigg\}\,,

(72)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{q}^{g}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Delta P_{gq}(z)+\Delta P_{gq}(z)\Big[-L+2\ln(z(1-z))\Big]-2(1-z)\bigg\}\,,

(73)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{g}^{q}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}T_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Delta{P}_{qg}(z)+\Delta P_{qg}(z)\Big[-L+2\ln(z(1-z))\Big]+2(1-z)\bigg\}\,,

(74)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{G}_{g}^{g}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{A}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta P_{gg}(z)+\frac{\beta_0}{2 C_A} \delta(1-z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}+\Delta P_{gg}(z)(-L+2\ln{z})-4(1-z)

\displaystyle\quad+4\Big[2(1-z)^{2}+z\Big]\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\bigg\}\,,

(75)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\mathscr{G}_{q}^{q}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg\{\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta_T P_{qq}(z)+\frac{3}{2} \delta(1-z)}+\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z)(-L+2\ln{z})+4z\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\bigg\}\,,

(76)

where we can eliminate all large logarithms
L
by choosing
\mu=\omega\tan(R / 2)
.
At the intermediate scale
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}\gg\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}}
, one can match the JFF
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
onto the longitudinally (transversely) polarized fragmentation functions
\Delta_{(T)}D_{j}^{h}(x,\mu)
as in
eq.

54
.
In order to perform the matching calculation and determine the coefficients
\mathscr{J}_{ij}
, we simply need the perturbative results of the fragmentation functions
\Delta_{(T)}D_{i}^{j}(x,\mu)
for a parton
i
fragmenting into a parton
j
.
The renormalized
\Delta_{(T)}D_{i}^{j}(x,\mu)
at
\order{\alpha_s}
using pure dimensional regularization are given by:

\displaystyle\Delta D_{q}^{q}(x,\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-x)+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta P_{qq}(x)+\frac{3}{2} \delta(1-x)}\,,

(77)

\displaystyle\Delta D_{q}^{g}(x,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Delta P_{gq}(x)\,,

(78)

\displaystyle\Delta D_{g}^{q}(x,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}T_{F}}{2\pi}\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Delta P_{qg}(x)\,,

(79)

\displaystyle\Delta D_{g}^{g}(x,\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-x)+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{A}}{2\pi}\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta P_{gg}(x) + \frac{\beta_0}{2 C_A} \delta(1-x)}\,,

(80)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}D_{q}^{q}(x,\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-x)+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\pqty{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\bqty{\Delta_T P_{qq}(x) + \frac{3}{2} \delta(1-x)}\,.

(81)

Using the results for
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{j}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
and
\Delta_{(T)}D_{i}^{j}(x,\mu)
, we obtain the following matching coefficients:

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{J}_{qq}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z)+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg[\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}-\Delta P_{qq}(z)L

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89418pt+(1-z)+\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{qq}^{\text{anti-}k_{T}}\bigg]\,,

(82)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{J}_{qg}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg[-\Delta P_{gq}(z)L-2(1-z)+\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{qg}^{\text{anti-}k_{T}}(z)\bigg]\,,

(83)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{J}_{gq}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}T_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg[-\Delta P_{qg}(z)L+2(1-z)+\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{gq}^{\text{anti-}k_{T}}(z)\bigg]\,,

(84)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathscr{J}_{gg}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z)+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{A}}{2\pi}\bigg[\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}-\Delta P_{gg}(z)L

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89418pt-4(1-z)+\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{gg}^{\text{anti-}k_{T}}(z)\bigg]\,,

(85)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\mathscr{J}_{qq}(\omega,R,z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z)+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\bigg[\delta(1-z)\pqty{\frac{L^2}{2}-\frac{\pi^2}{12}}-\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z)L+\Delta_{T}\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{q}^{\text{anti-}k_{T}}\bigg]\,,

(86)

where
\Delta_{(T)}\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{ij}^{{\text{anti-}k_{T}}}(z)
are jet-algorithm dependent.
For anti-
k_{T}
jets, we have:

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{qq}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}

\displaystyle=2\Delta P_{qq}(z)\ln{z}+2\pqty{1+z^2}\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\,,

(87)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{qg}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}

\displaystyle=2\Delta P_{gq}(z)\Big[\ln(z(1-z))\Big]\,,

(88)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{gq}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}

\displaystyle=2\Delta P_{qg}(z)\Big[\ln(z(1-z))\Big]\,,

(89)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{gg}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}

\displaystyle=2\Delta P_{gg}(z)\ln{z}+4\Big[2(1-z)^{2}+z\Big]\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\,,

(90)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\hat{\mathscr{J}}_{qq}^{\text{anti}-k_{T}}

\displaystyle=2\Delta_{T}P_{qq}(z)\ln{z}+4z\pqty{\frac{\ln(1-z)}{1-z}}_{+}\,,

(91)

where the functions
\Delta_{(T)}\hat{P}_{ji}
have the following expressions

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{qq}(z)

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{2}{(1-z)_{+}}-1-z\right]\,,

(92)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{gq}(z)

\displaystyle=\left[2-z\right]\,,

(93)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{qg}(z)

\displaystyle=\left[2z-1\right]\,,

(94)

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{P}_{gg}(z)

\displaystyle=2\left[\frac{1}{(1-z)_{+}}-2z+1\right]\,,

(95)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\hat{P}_{qq}(z)

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{2z}{(1-z)_{+}}\right]\,,

(96)

The jet fragmentation function
\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)
satisfies the following RG equation

\displaystyle\mu\frac{\differential{}}{\differential{\mu}}\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)=\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)\,,

(97)

where the anomalous dimension
\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}(\mu)=\gamma_{J}^{i}(\mu)
is the same as that of the unmeasured jet
function
J_{i}((\omega,R,\mu)

[
JPW11
,
Waa12
,
EVW10
]
. The solution to the RG equation is then

\displaystyle\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}(\omega,R,z,\mu)=\Delta_{(T)}\mathscr{G}_{i}^{h}\left(\omega,R,z,\mu_{\mathscr{G}}\right)\exp\left[\int_{\mu_{\mathscr{G}}}^{\mu}\frac{\differential{\mu^{\prime}}}{\mu^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathscr{G}}^{i}\left(\mu^{\prime}\right)\right],

(98)

where the scale
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}
should be the characteristic scale that eliminates the large logarithms in the fixed-order perturbative calculations. In the large
z
region, the scale choice
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}=

\omega\tan(R / 2)(1-z)\equiv p_{TRZ}
resums
[
Waa12
]
both
\ln R
and
\ln(1-z)
. However, for consistency, this would require extracted fragmentation functions
\Delta_{(T)}D^{h/j}
with a built-in resummation of logarithms in
(1-z)
, which is currently not available. It might be instructive to point out that with such a scale, the power corrections in
eq.

54
will be of the order of
\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}}^{2}/\left[\omega^{2}\tan^{2}(R/2)(1-z)^{2}\right]
, similar to the usual threshold resummation, see, e.g. Ref.
[
BNP07
]
. For the numerical calculations presented in the next section, we will choose
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}=\omega\tan(R / 2)
to resum
\ln R
and comment on the effect of
\ln(1-z)
resummation.

3
Polarized transverse momentum dependent JFFs (TMDJFFs)

This section outlines the definition of TMD semi-inclusive jet fragmentation functions in SCET, which describe the distribution of hadron transverse momentum within a jet.
Both unpolarized and polarized fragmenting quarks are considered.
The operator definitions of these functions in SCET are presented, followed by their factorization formalism, which involves hard functions, soft functions, and TMDFFs.
The calculation of these functions is carried out to NLO at the parton level, and their RG evolution equations are derived and solved.

1
TMD JFFs in semi-inclusive jet productions

Figure 3
:
Illustration for the distribution of hadrons inside a jet with transverse momentum
{\bm{j}}_{\perp}
and azimuthal angle
\hat{\phi}_{h}
with respect to the jet axis.

In this section, we review the concept of polarized TMD jet fragmentation functions introduced in
[
KLZ20b
]
.
In
fig.

3
, where a quark-initiated jet is considered, a hadron
h
is observed inside the jet, carrying a longitudinal momentum fraction
z_{h}
of the jet and a transverse momentum
\bm{j}_{\perp}
with respect to the jet axis. Both unpolarized and polarized fragmenting quarks are considered in our theoretical framework.
The operator definitions of these functions in SCET are presented, followed by their factorization formalism, which involves hard functions, soft functions, and TMD FFs.

Based on the field and operator definitions introduced in
section

1
, we can define the general correlators for TMD JFFs initiated by quark or gluon as:

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/q}\left(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp},S_{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{z}{2N_{c}}\delta\left(z_{h}-\frac{\omega_{h}}{\omega_{J}}\right)

(99)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\left\langle 0\left|\delta\left(\omega-n_{a}\cdot\mathcal{P}\right)\delta^{2}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\perp}/z_{h}+\bm{j}_{\perp}\right)\chi_{n}(0)\right|(Jh)X\right\rangle\left\langle(Jh)X\left|\bar{\chi}_{n}(0)\right|0\right\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/g,\mu v}\left(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp},S_{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{z\omega}{(d-2)\left(N_{c}^{2}-1\right)}\delta\left(z_{h}-\frac{\omega_{h}}{\omega_{J}}\right)

(100)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\left\langle 0\left|\delta\left(p^{-}-n_{a}\cdot\mathcal{P}\right)\delta^{2}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\perp}/z_{h}+\bm{j}_{\perp}\right)\mathcal{B}_{n\perp}^{\mu}(0)\right|(Jh)X\right\rangle\left\langle(Jh)X\left|\mathcal{B}_{n\perp}^{v}(0)\right|0\right\rangle\,.

where the energy fractions
z
and
z_{h}
have been defined in
section

1
.

Next, one can parameterize the correlators in
eqs.

99
and
100
at the leading power:

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp},S_{h})

\displaystyle=\Delta^{h/q\,[\not{n}_{b}]}\frac{\not{n}_{a}}{2}-\Delta^{h/q\,[\not{n}_{b}\gamma_{5}]}\frac{\not{n}_{a}\gamma_{5}}{2}+\Delta^{h/q\,[in_{b,\nu}\sigma^{k\nu}\gamma_{5}]}\frac{i{n}_{a,\mu}\sigma^{k\mu}\gamma_{5}}{2}\,,

(101)

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/g,ij}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp},S_{h})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta_{T}^{ij}\Big(\delta_{T}^{kl}\Delta^{h/g,kl}\Big)-\frac{i}{2}\epsilon_{T}^{ij}\Big(i\epsilon_{T}^{kl}\Delta^{h/g,kl}\Big)+\hat{S}\Delta^{h/g,ij}\,.

(102)

Here we have defined
\Delta^{h/q[\Gamma]}\equiv\frac{1}{4}\Tr(\Delta^{h/q} \Gamma)
.
The three terms on the r.h.s. of
eq.

101
include TMD JFFs with unpolarized, longitudinally polarized, and transversely polarized initial quarks. More details have been presented in our previous work
[
KLZ20b
]
. In the following context, we use
n
to represent light-cone vector
n_{b}
for simplification and provide the parametrization of quark TMD JFFs

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/q[\not{n}]}=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\epsilon^{ij}_{T}j_{\perp}^{i}S_{h\perp}^{j}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{D}_{1T}^{\perp h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\,,

(103)

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/q[\not{n}\gamma_{5}]}=

\displaystyle\,\Lambda_{h}\mathcal{G}_{1L}^{h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{h\perp}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{G}_{1T}^{h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\,,

(104)

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/q[in_{\nu}\sigma^{i\nu}\gamma_{5}]}=

\displaystyle\,S_{h\perp}^{i}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\epsilon^{ij}_{T}j_{\perp}^{j}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{j_{\perp}^{i}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\Lambda_{h}{\mathcal{H}_{1L}^{\perp h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle+\frac{j_{\perp}^{i}\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{h\perp}-\frac{1}{2}\bm{j}_{\perp}^{2}S_{h\perp}^{i}}{z_{h}^{2}M_{h}^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{\perp h/q}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\,,

(105)

U

L

T

U

\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/q}

\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp h/q}

L

\mathcal{G}^{h/q}_{1L}

\mathcal{H}_{1L}^{h/q}

T

\mathcal{D}_{1T}^{\perp h/q}

\mathcal{G}^{h/q}_{1T}

\mathcal{H}^{h/q}_{1}
,
\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{\perp h/q}

Table 2
:
Interpretation of TMDJFFs for quarks. The rows indicate the hadron polarization — unpolarized (U), longitudinally polarized (L), transversely polarized (T). And the columns indicate the quark polarization accordingly.

As for the gluon TMD JFFs given in
eq.

100
, they are parametrized as

\displaystyle\Delta^{h/g,\alpha\beta}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp},S_{h})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[-g_{T}^{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})+\frac{\bm{j}_{\perp}^{2}}{z_{h}^{2}M_{h}^{2}}\left(\frac{g_{T}^{\alpha\beta}}{2}+\frac{j_{\perp}^{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta}}{\bm{j}_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\Lambda_{h}}{2}\left[-i\epsilon_{T}^{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{G}_{1L}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})+\frac{\epsilon_{T}^{j_{\perp}\{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta\}}}{2z_{h}^{2}M_{h}^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{1L}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\bigg[g_{T}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\epsilon_{T}^{j_{\perp}S_{h\perp}}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{D}_{1T}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})+i\epsilon_{T}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{h\perp}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{G}_{1T}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\epsilon_{T}^{j_{\perp}\{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta\}}}{2z_{h}^{2}M_{h}^{2}}\frac{\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{h\perp}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\epsilon_{T}^{j_{\perp}\{\alpha}S_{h\perp}^{\beta\}}+\epsilon_{T}^{S_{h\perp}\{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta\}}}{4z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\bigg]\,.

(106)

Here functions
\mathcal{D}
,
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
on the r.h.s. of
eq.

106
represent the TMD JFFs with unpolarized, circularly polarized, and linearly polarized initial gluons respectively and we have adopted the notation
v_{T}^{\{\alpha}w_{T}^{\beta\}}=v_{T}^{\alpha}w_{T}^{\beta}+v_{T}^{\beta}w_{T}^{\alpha}
as applied in
[
MR01
,
Bou23
]
.
More specifically, one has each term in the r.h.s. of
eq.

106
give as

\displaystyle\delta_{T}^{\alpha\beta}\Delta^{h/g,\alpha\beta}=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\epsilon^{ij}_{T}j_{\perp}^{i}S_{h\perp}^{j}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\,,

(107)

\displaystyle i\epsilon_{T}^{\alpha\beta}\Delta^{h/g,\alpha\beta}=

\displaystyle\,\Lambda_{h}\mathcal{G}_{1L}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{h\perp}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{G}_{1T}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\,,

(108)

\displaystyle\hat{S}\Delta^{h/g,\alpha\beta}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{j_{\perp}^{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta}}{2z_{h}^{2}M_{h}^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\epsilon_{T}^{j_{\perp}\{\alpha}S_{h\perp}^{\beta\}}+\epsilon_{T}^{S_{h\perp}\{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta\}}}{8z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})

(109)

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon_{T}^{j_{\perp}\{\alpha}j_{\perp}^{\beta\}}}{4z_{h}^{2}M_{h}^{2}}\left(\Lambda_{h}\mathcal{H}_{1L}^{\perp,h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})-\frac{\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{h\perp}}{z_{h}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{\perp h/g}(z,z_{h},\bm{j}_{\perp})\right)\,,

(110)

where
\hat{S}O^{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\left(O^{\alpha\beta}+O^{\beta\alpha}-\delta_{T}^{\alpha\beta}O^{\rho\rho}\right)
and
\delta_{T}^{\alpha\beta}=-g_{T}^{\alpha\beta}
.

U

L

T

U

\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/g}

\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp h/g}

L

\mathcal{G}^{h/g}_{1L}

\mathcal{H}_{1L}^{h/g}

T

\mathcal{D}_{1T}^{\perp h/g}

\mathcal{G}^{h/g}_{1T}

\mathcal{H}^{h/g}_{1T}
,
\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{\perp h/g}

Table 3
:
Interpretation of TMDJFFs for gluons. The columns indicate the gluon polarization — unpolarized (U), circularly polarized (L), linearly polarized (T).

Since TMD JFFs represent the hadron fragmentation inside a fully reconstructed jet, their physical meaning is similar to that of standard TMD FFs as reviewed in
[
MV16b
]
.
Consequently, we adopt the calligraphic font of the letters used for the TMD FFs as the notations of TMD JFFs with corresponding polarizations.

In this study, following the unpolarized TMD JFFs calculation carried out in
[
KLR17
]
, we will focus on the kinematic region where
\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}}\lesssim j_{\perp}\ll p_{T}R
, where the standard collinear factorization breaks down due to the presence of the large logarithms of the form
\ln(p_T R / j_{\perp})
.
This situation necessitates the adoption of the TMD factorization
[
Col13
]
, which we will provide a detailed discussion of in the following sections.

TMD Factorization

In the kinematic region under consideration, the radiation relevant at leading power is restricted to collinear radiation within the jet, characterized by momentum that scales as
p_{c}=\pqty{p_c^- , p_c^+ , p_{c,T}}\sim p_{T}\pqty{1 , \lambda^2 , \lambda}
, where
\lambda\sim j_{\perp}/p_{T}
.
Additionally, soft radiation of order
j_{\perp}
is also relevant.
It is worth noting that harder emissions are only permitted outside the jet cone and will thus only impact the determination of the jet axis.
Consequently, the hadron transverse momentum
j_{\perp}
, which is defined with respect to the jet axis, remains intact from the radiations external to the jet.
A factorized formalism for the unpolarized TMD JFFs within SCET can thus be formulated as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}=

\displaystyle\,\hat{H}^{U}_{c\to i}\pqty{z, \omega_J R, \mu}\int\differential[2]{\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}}\differential[2]{\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\perp}}\delta^{2}\pqty{z_h \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\perp} + \boldsymbol{k}_{\perp} - \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}}

(111)

\displaystyle\times D_{1}^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}, \mu, \nu}S_{i}\pqty{\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\perp}, \mu, \nu R}\,,

where
S_{i}\pqty{\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\perp}, \mu, \nu R}
denotes the soft radiation.
The
\delta
function establishes a relationship between the hadron transverse momentum
\bm{j}_{\perp}
, relative to the jet axis, and two other momenta: the transverse component of soft radiation represented by
\bm{\lambda}_{\perp}
, and the hadron transverse momentum with respect to the soft radiation, denoted as
\bm{k}_{\perp}
.
Notice that
\bm{\lambda}_{\perp}
is multiplied by
z_{h}
to adjust for the dissimilarity between the fragmenting parton and the observed hadron.
As is common practice in TMD physics, we convert the aforementioned expression from the transverse momentum space to the coordinate
b
-space using the following transformation,

\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}=\hat{H}^{U}_{c\to i}\pqty{z, \omega_J R, \mu}\int\frac{\differential[2]{\boldsymbol{b}}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{i\bm{j}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{b}/z_{h}}\widetilde{D}_{1}^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu}\widetilde{S}_{i}(\bm{b},\mu,\nu R)\,,

(112)

where we have defined the Fourier transform for both
\widetilde{D}_{1}^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu}
and
\widetilde{S}_{i}(\bm{b},\mu,\nu R)
as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}_{1}^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{z_{h}^{2}}\int\differential[2]{\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{b}/z_{h}}D_{1}^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}, \mu, \nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{z_{h}^{2}}\int\differential{k_{\perp}}k_{\perp}2\pi J_{0}\pqty{\frac{b k_{\perp}}{z_h}}D_{1}^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{k}_\perp, \mu, \nu}\,,

(113)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{i}(\bm{b},\mu,\nu R)

\displaystyle=\int\differential[2]{\boldsymbol{\lambda_{\perp}}}e^{-i\bm{\lambda}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{b}}S_{i}\pqty{\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\perp}, \mu, \nu R}\,.

(114)

Now let us define the operator
\mathscr{C}
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}[\widetilde{D}^{h/i,(n)}]=\int\frac{b^{n+1}\differential{b}}{2\pi n!}\pqty{\frac{z_h^2 M_h^2}{j_{\perp}}}^{n}J_{n}\pqty{\frac{j_{\perp} b}{z_h}}\widetilde{D}^{h/i,(n)}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu}\widetilde{S}_{i}\pqty{\boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu R}\,,

(115)

where by default
n=0
, giving
\widetilde{D}^{h/i,(0)}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu}
the TMD FFs in the Fourier
b
-space.
This can be generalized to give the
n
-th moment:

\widetilde{D}^{h/i,(n)}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{b}, \mu, \nu}=\frac{1}{z_{h}^{2}}\frac{2\pi n!}{\pqty{z_h^2 M_h^2}^{n}}\int\differential{k_{\perp}}k_{\perp}\pqty{\frac{k_{\perp}}{b}}^{n}J_{n}\pqty{\frac{b k_{\perp}}{z_h}}D^{h/i}\pqty{z_h, \boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}, \mu, \nu}\,.

(116)

One can easily verify that with
n=0
and
D=D_{1}
,
eq.

116
gives
eq.

113
.
We can now write down the factorization for all the TMD JFFs as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}^{h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{U}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{D}_{1}^{h/i}}\,,

(117)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1T}^{\perp,h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{U}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{D}_{1T}^{\perp, h/i, (1)}}\,,

(118)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1L}^{h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{L}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{G}_{1L}^{h/i}}\,,

(119)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1T}^{h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{L}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{G}_{1T}^{h/i, (1)}}\,,

(120)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{T}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{H}_{1}^{h/i}}\,,

(121)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp,h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{T}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{H}_{1}^{\perp, h/i, (1)}}\,,

(122)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1L}^{\perp,h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{T}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{H}_{1L}^{\perp, h/i, (1)}}\,,

(123)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1T}^{\perp,h/c}\pqty{z, z_h, \omega_J R, \boldsymbol{j}_{\perp}, \mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{H}_{c\to i}^{T}(z,\omega_{J}R,\mu)\mathscr{C}\bqty{\widetilde{H}_{1T}^{\perp, h/i, (2)}}\,.

(124)

Here the superscripts
U
,
L
and
T
of
\hat{H}_{c\to i}
represent unpolarized, longitudinally polarized, or transversely polarized hard matching functions, and they will be provided in the next subsection.
The above equations also show how various TMD JFFs are matched onto their corresponding TMD FFs, with which the matching of the scenarios listed in
table

4
can be performed.
The soft functions are identical for all scenarios and can be found in
[
KLR17
,
KLS21b
]
.
The subsequent sections will provide detailed calculation of the hard matching functions and partonic TMD FFs, as well as their renormalization using RG evolutions.

,

D_{1T}^{\perp}

G_{1T}

H_{1}
,
